Have you ever thought that others might see you as normal despite how you feel?\n\nPatient: My wife, my daughter, and even my pastor have all tried to reassure me that I look normal and they don't see what I'm talking about. But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me."

Around the same time, he started attending more medical appointments and community health screenings, where he felt intensely scrutinized about his aging body, especially his facial skin and midsection, which he had long been privately self-conscious about but could previously ignore amid farm work and art. As he cut back on heavier farm labor due to joint pain and shifted to mentoring and grant writing, he spent more hours indoors, scrutinizing old photos and mirrors in his studio instead of being absorbed in physical work and community events, and he began turning down invitations to play guitar or exhibit his watercolors because he felt too \"embarrassed\" by how he looked. His wife eventually urged him to seek psychiatric help after he skipped a follow-up cardiology appointment and a long-planned family reunion because he was convinced others would be focused on his perceived physical flaws, despite reassurance from family and his pastor that nothing about his appearance had meaningfully changed.\n- Chief Complaint: “I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S067 (Preoccupation_with_Appearance): All day long my mind keeps circling back to how saggy and blotchy my face looks now and how my gut hangs over my belt, and I picture people at church or the clinic staring and thinking, ‘What happened to him?’ even though my wife swears she can’t see what I’m talking about.\n- S068 (Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors): I catch myself going back to the bathroom mirror every half hour, pulling at the skin on my cheeks and lifting my shirt to poke at my stomach, then changing shirts two or three times and asking my wife or daughter, ‘Does this make my belly look as bad as it really is?’ before I can even think about leaving the house.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started turning down farm meetings, church events, and chances to play music or show my paintings because I’m so ashamed of how I look, and even skipped a heart follow-up because I couldn’t stand the idea of the doctors and nurses seeing my face and stomach.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: “I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore.”\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Stressor / Background, S067 (Preoccupation_with_Appearance), S068 (Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors)\nAnswer strategy: Explain that to you, these flaws feel completely real, obvious, and unquestionable.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore. It’s just really hard to even leave the house because I feel so embarrassed by my appearance.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid going out, is it mainly because you fear other people will judge or embarrass you in social situations, or is it because you are specifically preoccupied with a perceived flaw in your appearance?\n\nPatient: It is definitely because of how I look. I am just so embarrassed by my face looking saggy and blotchy, and how my stomach looks, that I'm convinced people will stare right at those flaws and judge me. I just can't bear the thought of anyone seeing how bad I look now.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time checking, comparing, or trying to hide these specific appearance flaws, or do you mainly avoid people because you fear embarrassment in social situations in general?\n\nPatient: I spend so much time checking—I go to the bathroom mirror every half hour to pull at my cheek skin, poke my stomach, and change my shirt multiple times while asking my wife for reassurance. I've started avoiding church, farm meetings, and even my cardiology appointments because I'm just too ashamed of my face and stomach and dread people staring at those specific areas.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these appearance flaws are definitely real, or could they possibly be exaggerated by your mind? Have you ever thought that others might see you as normal despite how you feel?\n\nPatient: My wife, my daughter, and even my pastor have all tried to reassure me that I look normal and they don't see what I'm talking about. But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?"
  }

When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.

It’s just really hard to even leave the house because I feel so embarrassed by my appearance.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid going out, is it mainly because you fear other people will judge or embarrass you in social situations, or is it because you are specifically preoccupied with a perceived flaw in your appearance?\n\nPatient: It is definitely because of how I look. I am just so embarrassed by my face looking saggy and blotchy, and how my stomach looks, that I'm convinced people will stare right at those flaws and judge me. I just can't bear the thought of anyone seeing how bad I look now.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time checking, comparing, or trying to hide these specific appearance flaws, or do you mainly avoid people because you fear embarrassment in social situations in general?\n\nPatient: I spend so much time checking—I go to the bathroom mirror every half hour to pull at my cheek skin, poke my stomach, and change my shirt multiple times while asking my wife for reassurance. I've started avoiding church, farm meetings, and even my cardiology appointments because I'm just too ashamed of my face and stomach and dread people staring at those specific areas.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these appearance flaws are definitely real, or could they possibly be exaggerated by your mind? Have you ever thought that others might see you as normal despite how you feel?\n\nPatient: My wife, my daughter, and even my pastor have all tried to reassure me that I look normal and they don't see what I'm talking about. But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?\n\nPatient: It has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there. When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to question this belief at all, or is it completely fixed and impossible for you to doubt even a little?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I'm not really sure how to answer if it's completely fixed, but it just feels incredibly real and hard to ignore. The thoughts and feelings are just so overwhelming that it's difficult to even think about doubting them.

It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?\n\nPatient: It has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there. When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to question this belief at all, or is it completely fixed and impossible for you to doubt even a little?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer if it's completely fixed, but it just feels incredibly real and hard to ignore. The thoughts and feelings are just so overwhelming that it's difficult to even think about doubting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubt that these flaws might be exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely convinced they are truly there and obvious to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't have any doubts; I am completely convinced these flaws are real and highly visible. Even though my wife and pastor tell me they don't see anything, I just know people at church and the clinic are staring right at my saggy face and stomach. It feels far too obvious to just be something my mind is exaggerating."

But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?\n\nPatient: It has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there. When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to question this belief at all, or is it completely fixed and impossible for you to doubt even a little?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer if it's completely fixed, but it just feels incredibly real and hard to ignore. The thoughts and feelings are just so overwhelming that it's difficult to even think about doubting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubt that these flaws might be exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely convinced they are truly there and obvious to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't have any doubts; I am completely convinced these flaws are real and highly visible. Even though my wife and pastor tell me they don't see anything, I just know people at church and the clinic are staring right at my saggy face and stomach. It feels far too obvious to just be something my mind is exaggerating.\n\nDoctor: Besides the appearance concern, have you ever had other fixed beliefs that seem clearly untrue to others, such as being watched, followed, harmed, or receiving special messages?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any of those kinds of thoughts or feelings. My worries are strictly limited to how I look, especially my face and my stomach."
  }
]

Around the same time, he started attending more medical appointments and community health screenings, where he felt intensely scrutinized about his aging body, especially his facial skin and midsection, which he had long been privately self-conscious about but could previously ignore amid farm work and art. As he cut back on heavier farm labor due to joint pain and shifted to mentoring and grant writing, he spent more hours indoors, scrutinizing old photos and mirrors in his studio instead of being absorbed in physical work and community events, and he began turning down invitations to play guitar or exhibit his watercolors because he felt too \"embarrassed\" by how he looked. His wife eventually urged him to seek psychiatric help after he skipped a follow-up cardiology appointment and a long-planned family reunion because he was convinced others would be focused on his perceived physical flaws, despite reassurance from family and his pastor that nothing about his appearance had meaningfully changed.\n- Chief Complaint: “I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S067 (Preoccupation_with_Appearance): All day long my mind keeps circling back to how saggy and blotchy my face looks now and how my gut hangs over my belt, and I picture people at church or the clinic staring and thinking, ‘What happened to him?’ even though my wife swears she can’t see what I’m talking about.\n- S068 (Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors): I catch myself going back to the bathroom mirror every half hour, pulling at the skin on my cheeks and lifting my shirt to poke at my stomach, then changing shirts two or three times and asking my wife or daughter, ‘Does this make my belly look as bad as it really is?’ before I can even think about leaving the house.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started turning down farm meetings, church events, and chances to play music or show my paintings because I’m so ashamed of how I look, and even skipped a heart follow-up because I couldn’t stand the idea of the doctors and nurses seeing my face and stomach.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: “I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore.”\n- Answer only what the doctor asks. Do not list your symptoms unprompted.\n- Speak naturally and briefly, as a real patient would (2–3 sentences max).\n- Do not mention symptoms that the doctor has not asked about.\n- If asked about a symptom not in your profile, say you are not sure.\n- Respond in English.\n\n\n[This Turn: Profile Alignment Result — Follow Strictly]\n(Matched with profile)\nRelated sections: Chief Complaint, S067 (Preoccupation_with_Appearance), Stressor / Background\nAnswer strategy: Confirm that your worries are entirely focused on your physical appearance—specifically your face, skin, and stomach—and how embarrassing you think you look. Deny having any other unusual beliefs, such as being followed, watched, harmed, or receiving special messages, clarifying that your concern is strictly about people judging your looks."

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore. It’s just really hard to even leave the house because I feel so embarrassed by my appearance.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid going out, is it mainly because you fear other people will judge or embarrass you in social situations, or is it because you are specifically preoccupied with a perceived flaw in your appearance?\n\nPatient: It is definitely because of how I look. I am just so embarrassed by my face looking saggy and blotchy, and how my stomach looks, that I'm convinced people will stare right at those flaws and judge me. I just can't bear the thought of anyone seeing how bad I look now.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time checking, comparing, or trying to hide these specific appearance flaws, or do you mainly avoid people because you fear embarrassment in social situations in general?\n\nPatient: I spend so much time checking—I go to the bathroom mirror every half hour to pull at my cheek skin, poke my stomach, and change my shirt multiple times while asking my wife for reassurance. I've started avoiding church, farm meetings, and even my cardiology appointments because I'm just too ashamed of my face and stomach and dread people staring at those specific areas.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these appearance flaws are definitely real, or could they possibly be exaggerated by your mind? Have you ever thought that others might see you as normal despite how you feel?\n\nPatient: My wife, my daughter, and even my pastor have all tried to reassure me that I look normal and they don't see what I'm talking about. But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?\n\nPatient: It has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there. When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to question this belief at all, or is it completely fixed and impossible for you to doubt even a little?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer if it's completely fixed, but it just feels incredibly real and hard to ignore. The thoughts and feelings are just so overwhelming that it's difficult to even think about doubting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubt that these flaws might be exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely convinced they are truly there and obvious to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't have any doubts; I am completely convinced these flaws are real and highly visible. Even though my wife and pastor tell me they don't see anything, I just know people at church and the clinic are staring right at my saggy face and stomach. It feels far too obvious to just be something my mind is exaggerating.\n\nDoctor: Besides the appearance concern, have you ever had other fixed beliefs that seem clearly untrue to others, such as being watched, followed, harmed, or receiving special messages?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any of those kinds of thoughts or feelings. My worries are strictly limited to how I look, especially my face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard, seen, or felt things that other people do not, or had any experiences that made you think something unusual was happening beyond the appearance concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I have never heard, seen, or felt things that aren't really there, and nothing unusual like that has ever happened to me. My mind is entirely focused on my appearance concerns and how bad my face and stomach look.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other fixed beliefs that other people would consider clearly untrue, such as being watched, followed, harmed, or given special messages? Or is the concern only about your appearance?"
  }

No, I have never believed anything like that. My concern is strictly about my appearance, especially my face and my stomach, and how embarrassing it is for me to be seen. I only worry about people judging how I look.

His wife eventually urged him to seek psychiatric help after he skipped a follow-up cardiology appointment and a long-planned family reunion because he was convinced others would be focused on his perceived physical flaws, despite reassurance from family and his pastor that nothing about his appearance had meaningfully changed.\n- Chief Complaint: “I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S067 (Preoccupation_with_Appearance): All day long my mind keeps circling back to how saggy and blotchy my face looks now and how my gut hangs over my belt, and I picture people at church or the clinic staring and thinking, ‘What happened to him?’ even though my wife swears she can’t see what I’m talking about.\n- S068 (Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors): I catch myself going back to the bathroom mirror every half hour, pulling at the skin on my cheeks and lifting my shirt to poke at my stomach, then changing shirts two or three times and asking my wife or daughter, ‘Does this make my belly look as bad as it really is?’ before I can even think about leaving the house.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started turning down farm meetings, church events, and chances to play music or show my paintings because I’m so ashamed of how I look, and even skipped a heart follow-up because I couldn’t stand the idea of the doctors and nurses seeing my face and stomach.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint.     "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I can’t stop thinking about how bad I look, especially my face and stomach, and it’s gotten to where I don’t want people to see me anymore. It’s just really hard to even leave the house because I feel so embarrassed by my appearance.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid going out, is it mainly because you fear other people will judge or embarrass you in social situations, or is it because you are specifically preoccupied with a perceived flaw in your appearance?\n\nPatient: It is definitely because of how I look. I am just so embarrassed by my face looking saggy and blotchy, and how my stomach looks, that I'm convinced people will stare right at those flaws and judge me. I just can't bear the thought of anyone seeing how bad I look now.\n\nDoctor: Do you spend a lot of time checking, comparing, or trying to hide these specific appearance flaws, or do you mainly avoid people because you fear embarrassment in social situations in general?\n\nPatient: I spend so much time checking—I go to the bathroom mirror every half hour to pull at my cheek skin, poke my stomach, and change my shirt multiple times while asking my wife for reassurance. I've started avoiding church, farm meetings, and even my cardiology appointments because I'm just too ashamed of my face and stomach and dread people staring at those specific areas.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these appearance flaws are definitely real, or could they possibly be exaggerated by your mind? Have you ever thought that others might see you as normal despite how you feel?\n\nPatient: My wife, my daughter, and even my pastor have all tried to reassure me that I look normal and they don't see what I'm talking about. But when I look in the mirror, these flaws feel completely real and terrible, and I can't help but feel like everyone else will stare and judge me. It just feels so obvious to me.\n\nDoctor: When you avoid people, is it mainly because you fear being judged in social situations in general, or is it specifically because you are focused on these perceived flaws in your face and stomach?\n\nPatient: It is strictly because of how I look, not because I have a general fear of socializing. I actually used to love going to church, playing guitar, and attending farm meetings, but now I avoid them because I'm convinced people will stare at my saggy, blotchy face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think there might be a chance that your appearance concerns are being exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely certain that your face and stomach really look this bad to others?\n\nPatient: I am completely certain that they really do look that bad. My family and pastor try to reassure me, but I think they're just trying to spare my feelings. To me, the sagging in my face and my stomach are completely real, and I'm convinced everyone else will see them immediately.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other unusual beliefs, such as thinking someone is trying to harm you, watch you, or send you special messages, or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any thoughts like that about people trying to harm me, spy on me, or send me messages. My worries are entirely focused on how bad my face and stomach look, and the fear that everyone is staring at and judging my appearance.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed anything about your appearance that seems clearly untrue or impossible to other people, or has this always felt completely real and unquestionable to you?\n\nPatient: It has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there. When I look in the mirror, it is just too obvious, and I'm certain that others will stare at them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been able to question this belief at all, or is it completely fixed and impossible for you to doubt even a little?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer if it's completely fixed, but it just feels incredibly real and hard to ignore. The thoughts and feelings are just so overwhelming that it's difficult to even think about doubting them.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have any doubt that these flaws might be exaggerated by your mind, or are you completely convinced they are truly there and obvious to other people?\n\nPatient: I don't have any doubts; I am completely convinced these flaws are real and highly visible. Even though my wife and pastor tell me they don't see anything, I just know people at church and the clinic are staring right at my saggy face and stomach. It feels far too obvious to just be something my mind is exaggerating.\n\nDoctor: Besides the appearance concern, have you ever had other fixed beliefs that seem clearly untrue to others, such as being watched, followed, harmed, or receiving special messages?\n\nPatient: No, I have never had any of those kinds of thoughts or feelings. My worries are strictly limited to how I look, especially my face and my stomach.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard, seen, or felt things that other people do not, or had any experiences that made you think something unusual was happening beyond the appearance concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I have never heard, seen, or felt things that aren't really there, and nothing unusual like that has ever happened to me. My mind is entirely focused on my appearance concerns and how bad my face and stomach look.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other fixed beliefs that other people would consider clearly untrue, such as being watched, followed, harmed, or given special messages? Or is the concern only about your appearance?\n\nPatient: No, I have never believed anything like that. My concern is strictly about my appearance, especially my face and my stomach, and how embarrassing it is for me to be seen. I only worry about people judging how I look.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any time when you could recognize that these appearance concerns might be exaggerated or not actually true, even a little? Or have you always been completely certain that the flaws are real and obvious to others?"

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nIt has always felt completely real and unquestionable to me. Even though my wife, family, and pastor keep telling me that nothing about my appearance has changed and they can't see what I'm talking about, I remain absolutely convinced the flaws are there.
